2
General Setup For Topological Twisting

Since we wish our discussion to apply to non-Lagrangian theories, it is very useful to adopt the “functorial” formulation of QFT going back to the work of Segal in 2d conformal field theory
Seg88a ()
;
Seg88b ()
;
Seg (02)
and Atiyah in topological field theory
Ati (89)
.
8
8

8

Atiyah and Segal quote Feynman path integrals as their main motivation. However, as pointed out to us by Y. Tachikawa, the original papers of Schwinger
Sch (48)
and Tomonaga
Tom (46)
formulated relativistic field theory in terms of families of unitary operators
U(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})
associated to spatial slices
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}
and satisfying the basic axioms of the functorial formulation. One important difference is that in the older works, the theories are formulated in Minkowski spacetime, hence on invertible bordisms with a globally defined time direction, and without Wick rotation.

The reason is that these formulations make use of the
answer
to a path integral, without presupposing some representation in terms of dynamical fields governed by an action principle.
For recent reviews, see
Fre (13)
;
Fre (19)
.
In this approach, one defines a set of “fields”
\mathfrak{F}
- to be thought of as background fields.
9
9

9

Technically, we should consider “fields” to be a sheaf on
\mr{Man}_{4}^{\mr{op}}
, as explained in
FT (12)
;
FH (13)
.

One considers a bordism category
\mr{Bord}^{\mathfrak{F}}_{\leq 4}
of manifolds of dimension
\leq 4
equipped with such fields. Then a “theory” is a
choice
of
\mathfrak{F}
and a monoidal functor

\mr{Z}:\mr{Bord}^{\mathfrak{F}}_{\leq 4}\to\mr{VECT}~,

(3)

where
\mr{VECT}
is a category of vector spaces over
\mathbb{C}
.
10
10

10

In modern parlance we are considering a two-step theory. Eventually one wishes to have a fully extended, or fully local theory, but such considerations are far beyond the scope of this paper. Moreover, a fully rigorous formulation for the kinds of interacting field theories we will consider has not been formulated. Rather this is a topic of current research. See
KS (21)
for a significant contribution to this current research. We are being deliberately vague about the precise nature of the vector spaces. They will need to be infinite-dimensional.

We stress that we regard the choice of fields, and hence the domain bordism category for
\mr{Z}
as a
choice
that one makes in defining the theory.

Specializing now to the class of
d=4

{\cal N}=2
field theories the most obvious choice for the fields, which we denote as
\widetilde{{\cal F}}
,
evaluated on a fixed manifold
{\mathbb{X}}
includes the supermanifold of field configurations of
d=4

{\cal N}=2
conformal supergravity coupled to
{\cal N}=2
vectormultiplets for the ‘‘flavor’’ symmetries.
11
11

11

For general backgrounds involving
{\cal N}=2
conformal supergravity, it is natural to also consider couplings to background
{\cal N}=2
hypermultiplets, but we will not do so.

This viewpoint (which was important in
CMRS (23)
) is too general for our present purposes, so we will restrict attention to a submanifold where many of the fields in the conformal supergravity multiplet have been set to zero. For our purposes, the fields will include the orientation of
{\mathbb{X}}
together with the groupoid of principal
\widetilde{G}
-bundles with connection for the Lie group

\widetilde{G}=\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{SU(2)_{R}}\times G^{\mr}{f}~,

(4)

where the group
G^{\mr}{f}
is often called the “flavor” group. It is the global internal symmetry group that commutes with supersymmetry.

Let
p_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
denote the projection to the three factors
of the group (
4
):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Spin(4)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{SU(2)_{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\mathsf{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{3}}

(5)

Denote the principal
\widetilde{G}
-bundle with connection over
{\mathbb{X}}
by
(\widetilde{P},\widetilde{\bm{\nabla}})
. The
G^{\mr}{f}
component of the connection, that is,
12
12

12

We remind the reader that the transfer of structure group and connection is described in Appendix
B
.

the connection
p_{3,*}(\widetilde{P},\widetilde{\bm{\nabla}})
, should be enhanced to a full
d=4

{\cal N}=2
vectormultiplet. The factor
\mr{Spin(4)}
is to be regarded as the spin covering of the
\mr{SO(4)}
structure group of the bundle of oriented frames of
T{\mathbb{X}}
,
so
\pi_{{\mathbb{X}},*}(\widetilde{P})=\mr{OFr}(T{\mathbb{X}})
where
\pi_{{\mathbb{X}}}
is a projection on the first factor followed by the standard quotient homomorphism
\mr{Spin(4)}\to\mr{SO(4)}
. Thus we will begin
with
d=4

{\cal N}=2
theories viewed as a functor

\widetilde{\mr{Z}}:\mr{Bord}^{\widetilde{{\cal F}}}_{\leq 4}\to\mr{VECT}~.

(6)

Since this is a functor,
\widetilde{\mr{Z}}
will define state spaces. On
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
with Euclidean metric and zero background fields for
\mr{SU(2)_{R}}\times G^{\mr}{f}
, the state space will be a unitary representation of the
d=4

{\cal N}=2
Poincaré group. Related to this, the local operators of the theory should be in
d=4

{\cal N}=2
Poincaré multiplets.

Note that the domain category of (
6
) only admits 4-manifolds
{\mathbb{X}}
with a spin structure.
The domain category of the theory can be enlarged. Put differently, the theory can be extended to include non-spin manifolds as follows. Consider a subgroup of the center of
\widetilde{G}
, written

C\subset Z(\widetilde{G})
. Define
{\cal F}
to be the groupoid of principal
G:=\widetilde{G}/C
bundles with connection. We say the theory “extends” to a theory

\mr{Z}:\mathsf{Bord}_{\leq 4}^{{\cal F}}\to\mathsf{VECT}~,

(7)

if the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Bord}_{\leq 4}^{\widetilde{{\cal F}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{VECT}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Bord}_{\leq 4}^{{\cal F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mr{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mr{Z}}

(8)

commutes (up to isomorphism of functors). Here
\pi_{*}:\mathsf{Bord}_{\leq 4}^{\widetilde{{\cal F}}}\to\mathsf{Bord}_{\leq 4}^{{\cal F}}
is transfer of structure group using the natural quotient homomorphism

\pi:\widetilde{G}\to G~.

A necessary and sufficient condition for this factorization is that the full functor
\widetilde{\mr{Z}}
be
C
-invariant. If we just examine the partition function then we note that
C
acts as a group of automorphisms on the principal
\widetilde{G}
-bundles with connection over
{\mathbb{X}}
. The action on the connection is via a center symmetry: Holonomies are multiplied by central elements. Therefore it acts on the partition function
\widetilde{\mr{Z}}
considered as a function of the bundle with connection. The condition is

c\cdot\widetilde{\mr{Z}}=\widetilde{\mr{Z}}\qquad\forall~c\in C~.

(9)

There can be many choices of
C
. Each choice of
C
defines a different “theory.” A maximal choice is one such that all theories extend to that choice. If
G^{\mr}{f}
is semisimple, the center is a finite Abelian group, so there exists a maximal subgroup.

{remark}

An analogy might clarify our reasoning here. Suppose we are given the partition function of a theory of chiral fermions on
2n
-dimensional Riemannian spin manifolds where
n
is an integer. But we are given this mathematical structure without being told what fields were used to compute that partition function. How could we deduce that the theory does not extend to non-spin manifolds? Thus, we are given, for every
2n
-dimensional spin manifold a Hermitian line bundle over the space of Riemannian metrics on that manifold, and a section of that line bundle. (In fact, the line bundle is the determinant line bundle
\mr{DET}(\not{D}^{+})
of the chiral Dirac operator with its Quillen metric, but we are not given that information because we do not know the fields.) We are given the information that the line bundle depends on a spin structure and we are given the action of the center symmetry. The center of the spin group acts as a group of automorphisms on the spin bundles, and this induces an action with character
\chi
acting on sections of the line bundle. If the action is nontrivial then we cannot extend to nonspin manifolds. Indeed, for
\mr{DET}(\not{D}^{+})
the action of
-1
is given by
\chi(-1)=(-1)^{\mathsf{Ind}(\not{D}^{+})}
and is nontrivial. In this way, we see that for a chiral fermion, the functor does not factorize through non-spin manifolds, even though we have not been informed of the nature of the fields. (A more elaborate argument applies to Dirac fermions.) The point of these remarks is that the same reasoning applies to theories where we do not know the fundamental fields, such as the non-Abelian 6d (2,0) theory or generic 4d
{\cal N}=2
theories of class
{\cal S}
.

{remark}

It is important to remark that the invariance condition on the partition function
is in principle less stringent than the invariance condition on the full functor. In this paper, we are primarily concerned with the partition function on compact manifolds.

In the application to four-manifold invariants, we definitely wish to include manifolds
{\mathbb{X}}
which are not spinnable. Therefore we wish to have principal
G
-bundles over an
{\mathbb{X}}
which is nonspinnable. This implies the necessary condition:

p_{1}(C)=\langle(-1,-1)\rangle\subset Z(\mr{Spin(4)})~,

(10)

where we have used
\mr{Spin(4)}\cong\mr{SU(2)}\times\mr{SU(2)}
.

Different theories will have different local operator content, but one supermultiplet that is present in all
d=4

{\cal N}=2
local QFTs is the energy-momentum supermultiplet. See
CDI16a ()
;
CDI16b ()
;
Ebe (20)
for details
and Appendix
D
for a quick summary.
In order for this multiplet to be invariant under
C
we must also have

(p_{1}\times p_{2})(C)=\langle(-1,-1,-1)\rangle\subset Z(\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{SU(2)_{R}})~.

(11)

The original example
Wit (88)
had
G^{\mr}{f}=1
and was based on a choice of background metric and
R
-symmetry gauge field obtained by transfer of structure group using a crucial homomorphism

\displaystyle\varphi^{\mr}{W}:\mr{SO(4)}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{SU(2)_{R}})/\langle(-1,-1,-1)\rangle~.

(12)

To write it, consider
\mr{SO(4)}
as the quotient
\mr{Spin(4)}/{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
where

\mr{Spin(4)}\cong\mr{SU(2)}\times\mr{SU(2)}
and

{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\cong\langle(-1,-1)\rangle\cong\mr{diag}({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\cong Z(\mr{Spin(4)})~.

(13)

Thus, an element of
\mr{SO(4)}
can be denoted as
[(u_{1},u_{2})]
where
square brackets indicate an equivalence class and
u_{1},u_{2}\in\mr{SU(2)}
.
Then we can write the homomorphism (
12
) as:

\varphi^{\mr}{W}\big([(u_{1},u_{2})]\big):=[(u_{1},u_{2}),u_{2}]~,

(14)

where the superscript
\mr{W}
stands for Witten and will be called the
Witten homomorphism
because it was essential in the original construction of

Wit (88)
.

The specification of background fields will be done via
transfer of structure group along a homomorphism

\varphi^{\mr{tw,bck}}:\underbrace{(\mr{Spin(4)}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}})/C^{\mr{tw,bck}}}_{G^{\mr{tw,bck}}}\rightarrow(\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{SU(2)_{R}}\times G^{\mr}{f})/C~,

(15)

where
C^{\mr{tw,back}}
is a subgroup of the center of
\mr{Spin(4)}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}
.
If we take
{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr}{f}
to be a maximal torus of
G^{\mr}{f}
then we can consider a homomorphism

\widetilde{\varphi}^{\mr{tw,bck}}:\mr{Spin(4)}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}\rightarrow(\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{SU(2)_{R}}\times G^{\mr}{f})/C~,

(16)

defined by

\widetilde{\varphi}^{\mr{tw,bck}}((u_{1},u_{2}),\mu)=[(u_{1},u_{2}),u_{2},\mu]~.

(17)

and then simply take
C^{\mr{tw,bck}}
to be the kernel of
\widetilde{\varphi}^{\mr{tw,bck}}
.
This is sufficient to be able to define a twisting for all theories.
(For special values of the masses, there will be special twistings.)
Examples such as the case of
{\cal N}=2^{*}
theories teach us that
the existence of a homomorphism to a quotient group such that

C\cdot\widetilde{\mr{Z}}=\widetilde{\mr{Z}}
requires that, in general,
C
must intersect
G^{\mr}{f}
nontrivially.

Other twisting homomorphisms are possible but they are constrained by the requirement that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\mr{tw,bck}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{G}/C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mr{Spin(4)}/\langle(-1,-1)\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{SU(2)_{R}})/\langle(-1,-1,-1)\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\mr{tw,bck}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}\times p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\mr{W}}}

(18)

commutes. This is indeed satisfied by the construction above.

Note that if
p_{1}
is the projection on the first factor of
G^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}
then we should impose

(p_{1})_{*}(P^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}})\cong(\mr{OFr}(T{\mathbb{X}}),\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{LC}})~.

(19)

When
G^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}
is of the form given in (
15
)
with
C^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}
a finite abelian subgroup of the center
we say that such a principal bundle
P^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}
with structure group
G^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}
defines a
generalized spin
{}^{\mr}{c}
structure
on
{\mathbb{X}}
. Morever, a connection
\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}
satisfying (
19
)
is a
generalized spin
{}^{\mr}{c}
connection
. We elaborate on this definition and give some examples in Appendix
C
. We will meet this condition again, in the context of Lagrangian theories in equation (
34
).

Returning to the
d=4

{\cal N}=2
energy-momentum multiplet, the relation (see (
322
))

\{{\cal Q}_{\alpha A},G^{B}_{\mu\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}\}=\sigma^{\nu}_{\alpha\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}\delta_{A}{}^{B}T_{\mu\nu}~,

(20)

implies that, in the presence of background fields obtained by transfer of structure group
\varphi^{\mr{tw,bck}}
satisfying
(
18
), there will be a vector-field
{\cal Q}
on the super-manifold of field configurations such that
13
13

13

Note that (
21
) is obtained from the second equation of (
322
) because in our conventions, the scalar supercharge
{\cal Q}
is obtained from the
right-handed
spinor supercharge of the
d=4

{\cal N}=2
supersymmetry algebra. We are being slightly schematic here to avoid distracting notation.

T_{\mu\nu}=\{{\cal Q},\Lambda_{\mu\nu}\}~.

(21)

A standard argument, going back to
Wit (88)
, shows
that (
21
) leads to the “topological invariance” of
\mr{Z}
. “Topological invariance” means independence of the choice of Riemannian metric. The reason is that the derivative of
\mr{Z}
with respect to the metric
g_{\mu\nu}
is given by
\mr{Z}
with an insertion of
T_{\mu\nu}
. If there is no BRST anomaly then insertions of
{\cal Q}
-exact operators must vanish. (As noted above, there
can
be BRST anomalies, leading to metric dependence, when
b_{2}^{+}({\mathbb{X}})\leq 1
.)

We will refer to theories with domain
\mathsf{Bord}_{\leq 4}^{{\cal F}}
such that, for all manifolds and background fields in the domain there is a
{\cal Q}
-symmetry, as “twisted theories.”

Similar reasoning shows that a twisted theory has a partition function that is invariant under continuous deformation of the other components of the
G^{\mr{tw,bck}}
connection. Using the general structure of the conserved current multiplets (which couple to the flavor symmetry connections) the general equation (see (
320
) and (
321
))

\delta_{A}^{~B}\sigma^{\mu}_{\alpha\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}J^{a}_{\mu}=\big\{{\cal Q}_{A\alpha},\widetilde{\psi}^{Ba}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}\big\}~,

(22)

becomes, after topological twisting

J_{\mu}^{a}=\{{\cal Q},\Lambda^{a}_{\mu}\}~.

(23)

Here the subscript
a
is an adjoint index in the Lie algebra of the symmetry.
Therefore, in the absence of a BRST anomaly, the derivative of the partition function with respect to the connection vanishes. Therefore, the twisted partition function only depends on the three pieces of topological data mentioned in the introduction.

{remark}

On the terminology “physical theory” vs. “twisted theory.”

We will use the (common) terminology of “twisted theory” and “physical theory” in our discussion below. Since we are regarding a “theory” as a functor
\mr{Z}
, part of the very definition of a “theory” involves a specification of the domain and codomain categories. As we have just mentioned, a “twisted theory” has a
{\cal Q}
-symmetry for all objects in the domain category. By a “physical theory” we mean a theory where this is not true for all objects in the domain category, although whether these should be considered more “physical” is a debatable point, best left to philosophers.

{remark}

In the interest of simplicity, our general discussion here has been limited to “zero-form symmetries.” It should be extended to include other generalized symmetries. We will indeed do this in the special case of Lagrangian theories in
sections
3
–
7
.

3
Fields For Lagrangian Theories

In this section, we describe the fields that are needed to define the exponentiated action
e^{-S}
.
We regard the exponentiated action on
{\mathbb{X}}
as the partition function on
{\mathbb{X}}

of an invertible field theory
FM (04)
.
We restrict attention to general renormalizable
d=4

{\cal N}=2
Lagrangian gauge theories. To define these one makes the following choice of basic data:

1.

\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}
: A semisimple simply connected compact Lie group. For simplicity, we will assume it is connected. The gauge group
G^{\mr{gauge}}
will be a finite quotient of
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}
, as explained below.

2.

\rho:\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}\to\mr{End}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\cal R})
: a quaternionic representation on a real
vector space
{\cal R}
equipped with a
G^{\mr{gauge}}
-invariant positive inner product.
The group of orthogonal hyperkähler isometries is denoted
\mr{O}(\rho)
. This data defines a compact Lie group
G^{\mr{f}}
– the
flavor group
– which is defined to be the commutant of
\rho(\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}})
in
\mr{O}(\rho)
.

3.

C^{\mr{phys}}
: an “admissible” subgroup
of the center of

\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}}:=\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{SU(2)}_{\mr{R}}\times G^{\mr{f}}\times\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}~.

(24)

The term “admissible” is defined by conditions (
29
) and (
31
) below.
There are different choices of admissible groups
C^{\mr{phys}}
, and they correspond to different theories.

The set of fields for the theory will include a principal bundle with connection over
{\mathbb{X}}
for the structure group
14
14

14

Our discussion at this point has some tangential overlap with recent discussions
HLL+ (22)
;
BI (23)
.

G^{\mr{phys}}:=\big(\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{SU(2)}_{\mr{R}}\times G^{\mr{f}}\times\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}\big)/C^{\mr{phys}}=\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}}/C^{\mr{phys}}~.

(25)

We denote the principal bundle with connection by

(P^{\mr{phys}}\to{\mathbb{X}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{phys}})~.

(26)

If we think of the invertible field theory (a.k.a. the exponentiated action
e^{-S}
) as a functor

\mr{Z}:\mr{Bord}_{\leq 4}^{{\cal F}}\to\mr{VECT}~,

(27)

then
{\cal F}({\mathbb{X}})
includes the groupoid of principal
G^{\mr{phys}}
-bundles with connection.

The bundle with connection
(P^{\mr{phys}}\to{\mathbb{X}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{phys}})
contains several pieces of physical data which are usually thought of as independent, but they are not completely independent because of the quotient by the finite group
C^{\mr{phys}}
.

Let
p_{i}
,
i=1,2,3,4
denote the projection to each of the factors of
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}}
. We have a transfer of structure group for bundles for each of the image groups
p_{i}(G^{\mr{phys}})
. We denote these by:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mr{Fr}({\mathbb{X}})&:=(p_{1})_{*}(P^{\mr{phys}})~,\\
P^{\mr{R}}&:=(p_{2})_{*}(P^{\mr{phys}})~,\\
P^{\mr{f}}&:=(p_{3})_{*}(P^{\mr{phys}})~,\\
P^{\mr{gauge}}&:=(p_{4})_{*}(P^{\mr{phys}})~,\end{split}

(28)

and refer to them respectively as the frame bundle,
R
-symmetry bundle, flavor bundle, and gauge bundle.

We are now ready to define the notion that
C^{\mr{phys}}
is
admissible
as follows:

1.

We can rederive the condition (
11
) as follows. First of all, we have:

(p_{1}\times p_{2})(C^{\mr{phys}})\subset\langle(-1,-1,-1)\rangle\subset Z(\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{SU(2)_{R}})\cong{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{3}~.

(29)

The reason is that vectormultiplet fields must be singlets under
the action of
C^{\mr{phys}}
. They are in the adjoint representation of the gauge group and hence are singlets under the center of that group. Moreover, they are singlets under the action of the flavor group.
Finally, we will want to identify
\mr{Spin(4)}/p_{1}(C^{\mr{phys}})\cong\mr{SO(4)}
with the structure group of
\mr{OFr}({\mathbb{X}})
, the bundle of oriented frames on
T{\mathbb{X}}
. If we wish the domain bordism category to include non-spin manifolds then we must have that

(p_{1}\times p_{2})({C^{\mr{phys}}})=\langle(-1,-1,-1)\rangle~.

(30)

2.

If there are hypermultiplets in the theory then

C^{\mr{phys}}
must also be a subgroup of the group:

\displaystyle\begin{split}C^{\mr{phys}}_{\mr{max}}&:=\{(\zeta,\zeta,\zeta,\mu,g)~|~\zeta\mu\rho(g)=\mathbbm{1}_{{\rm End}_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\cal R})}\}\\
&\qquad\subset Z(\mr{Spin(4)})\times Z(\mr{SU(2)}_{\mr{R}})\times Z(G^{\mr{f}})\times Z(\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}})~,\end{split}

(31)

where
\zeta\in\{\pm 1\}\cong{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
acts on
{\cal R}
by scalar multiplication.
The reason for this condition is that fields in the hypermultiplet representation
\rho
must be singlets under

C^{\mr{phys}}
(and we have used condition (
29
)). Note that this forces
C^{\mr{phys}}
to be a finite Abelian group. We will call
C^{\mr{phys}}_{\mr{max}}
the
maximal admissible subgroup
.

We make a few remarks on the bundles appearing in (
28
):

•

The gauge bundle
P^{\mr{gauge}}
is a principal
G^{\mr{gauge}}\cong\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}/C^{\mr{grb}}
bundle, where we define

C^{\mr{grb}}:=p_{4}(C^{\mr{phys}})~.

(32)

The reason for this notation is explained in item
4
below.

•

The bundle
\mr{Fr}({\mathbb{X}})
is a principal
\mr{SO(4)}
bundle because

\mr{Spin(4)}/p_{1}(C^{\mr{phys}})\cong\mr{SO(4)}~.

(33)

Moreover, if we use the transfer of structure group associated to the canonical homomorphism
15
15

15

We define
p_{i}(G^{\mr{phys}}):=p_{i}(\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}})/p_{i}(C^{\mr{phys}})
.

p_{1}:G^{\mr{phys}}\to\mr{SO(4)}\cong\mr{Spin(4)}/\langle(-1,-1)\rangle
then we require:

(p_{1})_{*}(P^{\mr{phys}}\to{\mathbb{X}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{phys}})\cong(\mr{OFr}(T{\mathbb{X}}),\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{LC}})~,

(34)

where
\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{LC}}
is the torsion-free (Levi-Civita) connection associated to a Riemannian metric on
{\mathbb{X}}
.

•

The
R
-symmetry bundle
P^{\mr{R}}
is a principal
\mr{SO(3)}_{\mr{R}}
bundle.

•

The flavor bundle
P^{\mr{f}}
is a principal
G^{\mr{f}}/p_{3}(C^{\mr{phys}})
bundle.

{remark}

1.

For generic hypermultiplet masses it is natural to replace
G^{\mr{f}}

with a maximal torus, so that that
P^{\mr{f}}
is in fact a
{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}/p_{3}(C^{\mr{phys}})
-bundle where
{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}\subset G^{\mr{f}}
is a maximal torus. In this case, we have restricted the background fields to reducible
G^{\mr{f}}
-connections and assumed a reduction of the structure group to
its maximal torus.

2.

The existence of
P^{\mr{phys}}
implies a restriction on characteristic classes of the “frame,” “
R
-symmetry,” “flavor,” and “gauge” bundles derived from
P^{\mr{phys}}
. See section
4
below.

The exponentiated action of the theory
e^{-S}
, regarded as an invertible field theory,
can be defined on a bordism category equipped with a set of fields
{\cal F}^{\mr{phys}}
that we informally write as:

{\cal F}^{\mr{phys}}:=\{\tau,m,(P^{\mr{phys}}\to{\mathbb{X}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{phys}}),\Phi^{\mr{phys}},b,\Phi^{\mr{grav}}\}~,

(35)

where

1.

\tau
is a
\mathbb{C}
-valued
\mathsf{Ad}(G^{\mr{gauge}}\times G^{\mr{f}})
-invariant bilinear form on
\mr{Lie}(G^{\mr{gauge}}\times G^{\mr{f}})
subject to the condition that the imaginary part is positive definite.
16
16

16

This follows from unitarity of the theory evaluated on Minkowski space.

\tau
enters the classical Lagrangian by defining the Yang-Mills couplings of the gauge theory. (
\tau
will be replaced by
\tau_{\mr{uv}}
or
\Lambda
below in the quantum theory.)

2.

m\in\mr{Lie}(G^{\mr{f}})\otimes\mathbb{C}
, subject to the condition that
[m,m^{\dagger}]=0
.
The field
m
enters the Lagrangian as
\langle m,\mu\rangle
where
\mu
is the hyperkähler
moment map for
G^{\mr{f}}
.
One should view
m
as the background vectormultiplet scalars
for
G^{\mr{f}}

3.

\Phi^{\mr{phys}}\in\Gamma(E_{\rho})
where
E_{\rho}
is an associated bundle to
P^{\mr{phys}}
constructed using
\rho
and the rules of supersymmetry for constructing vectormultiplets and hypermultiplets. Its sections include fermions, and hence, we should view the space of fields on
{\mathbb{X}}
as an infinite-dimensional supermanifold.
17
17

17

We will see later that, for suitable background fields, there is a fermionic vectorfield on this supermanifold derived from a scalar supersymmetry with
{\cal Q}^{2}=0
. The space of fields that define such an odd vectorfield is called the nilpotence variety. We will describe a subvariety of the nilpotence variety.

4.

The
b
-field is a flat
C^{\mr{grb}}
-gerbe connection
18
18

18

A gerbe with connection is (up to isomorphism) an element of a suitable differential cohomology group of degree 3. For a recent pedagogical introduction, see
Hit (99)
;
MS (24)
.

in a 5d topological field theory.
In order to handle properly the conditions on how one sums over ’t Hooft fluxes it is important to invoke the quiche picture of finite
“categorical symmetry” in field theory. See
FMT (22)
and references therein.
Example 3.15 of
FMT (22)
is particularly relevant, and an elaboration of the relevant issues can be found in
Fre (22)
. One must consider the 4d theory we discuss in this paper as a right boundary theory (on
\{1\}\times{\mathbb{X}}
) for a 5d
\pi
-finite topological field theory with target
B^{2}C^{\mr{grb}}
on
[0,1]\times{\mathbb{X}}
. There is a field in the right boundary theory which is an isomorphism of the
restriction of
b
with the gerbe describing the obstruction to lifting
(p_{4})_{*}(P^{\mr{phys}})
to a principal
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}
-bundle over
{\mathbb{X}}
. This is the origin of the notation
C^{\mr{grb}}
introduced in equation (
32
) above. We denote the characteristic class by
\mu(b)\in H^{2}({\mathbb{X}},C^{\mr{grb}})
.
There is a topological boundary condition
\rho
(possibly with the insertion of topological defects)
at the boundary
\{0\}\times{\mathbb{X}}
. Depending on the choice of
\rho
we might or might
not sum over ’t Hooft fluxes in computing physical quantities.
19
19

19

The pullback of
b
to
{\mathbb{X}}
itself can have automorphism so there can be several inequivalent isomorphisms to the gauge theory gerbe on the right (physical) boundary. Therefore we should also sum over
H^{1}({\mathbb{X}},C^{\mr{grb}})
.

We note that we see no obvious reason why one should not take
b
to be a gerbe for the full group
C^{\mr{phys}}

and indeed this generalization will be needed in Appendix
G
. We have not investigated this generalization systematically and doing so might be an interesting line of future enquiry. It might well streamline the presentation in this paper.

5.

\Phi^{\mr{grav}}
is short hand for various “gravitational” fields. The Riemannian metric is already encoded in the
connection
\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{phys}}
, but we must also choose an orientation of
{\mathbb{X}}
, and a truly complete story would include all the fields of background supergravity, including the odd partners of the metric. We will not go into details here, although some relevant constructions can be found in
CMRS (23)
.

6.

Explicit formulae for
e^{-S}
as a function of these fields can be extracted from the standard references:
GGRS (83)
;
DF (99)
;
dWLVP (85)
;
FVP (12)
.

{remark}

It is sometimes said that, since the supersymmetric gauge theory contains fermions, it must be defined on a spin manifold. Then, after twisting, the twisted theory can also be defined on non-spin manifolds. From the viewpoint of the present paper that reasoning is incorrect.
In defining a theory one must specify the domain bordism category. Different choices of group
C^{\mr{phys}}
lead to different choices of bordism category. For example, if we choose
C^{\mr{phys}}
to be trivial then the domain bordism category must contain only spin manifolds. However, if we choose
C^{\mr{phys}}
so that
p_{1}\times p_{2}
in equation
(
29
) is surjective then the domain category might include all standard four-manifolds.
This is just as true for the “physical theory” with general background fields as it is for the twisted theory defined below. (Related remarks can be found in
section 2.4.2 of
CD (18)
, and in
BI (23)
.)

{remark}

We have not been careful with the global structure of the space of fields in
(
35
). In fact, the criterion (
53
) which
connects the characteristic class of the (4d) boundary value of the (5d) gerbe
b
with a characteristic class of the flavor gauge fields suggests that there is a nontrivial fibration, and perhaps even a useful 2-group structure
CDI (18)
. We leave this for future investigation.

4
Cohomological Conditions For The Existence Of
P^{\mr{phys}}

In this section, we show that the existence of the bundle
P^{\mr{phys}}
in equation (
26
) above implies cohomological conditions linking the background gravity, flavor, and gerbe data.

Let us assume that the
G^{\mr{phys}}
-bundle
P^{\mr{phys}}\to{\mathbb{X}}
exists. We choose a good cover of
{\mathbb{X}}
, denoted by
\left\{U_{p}\right\}_{p\in{\cal P}}
, where
{\cal P}
is some index set.
Therefore, on double overlaps
U_{pq}:=U_{p}\cap U_{q}
, we have transition functions,

t_{pq}^{\mr{phys}}:U_{pq}\longrightarrow G^{\mr{phys}}~,

(36)

that satisfy the cocycle condition on triple overlaps
U_{pqr}:=U_{p}\cap U_{q}\cap U_{r}
,

t_{pq}^{\mr{phys}}(x)t_{qr}^{\mr{phys}}(x)t_{rp}^{\mr{phys}}(x)=\mathds{1}_{G^{\mr{phys}}}~,\quad\forall\,x\in U_{pqr}~.

(37)

We may choose the following transition functions for the bundles

(p_{i})_{*}(P^{\mr{phys}})
of equation (
28
) on overlaps:

\begin{split}\left(\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,+},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,-}\right):U_{pq}&\longrightarrow\mr{Spin(4)}\cong\mr{SU(2)}\times\mr{SU(2)}~,\\
\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{R}}:U_{pq}&\longrightarrow\mr{SU(2)_{R}}~,\\
\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{f}}:U_{pq}&\longrightarrow\mathbb{T}^{\mr{f}}~,\\
\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,g}:U_{pq}&\longrightarrow\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}~,\end{split}

(38)

such that
20
20

20

The equivalence class
[\cdot]
in (
39
) arises from the quotient by
C^{\mr{phys}}
, see (
25
).

t_{pq}^{\mr{phys}}=\left[\left(\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,+},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,-},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{R}},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{f}},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,g}\right)\right]~.

(39)

Note that our choice is ambiguous by

\left(\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,+},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,-},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{R}},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{f}},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,g}\right)\sim\left(\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,+},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,-},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{R}},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{f}},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,g}\right)\cdot\alpha~,

(40)

where
\alpha\in C^{\mr{phys}}
. Therefore, the functions (
38
)
need not individually satisfy the cocycle conditions. We can therefore define Čech-cocycles

\displaystyle\zeta_{pqr}^{\pm}:=\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\pm}\widetilde{t}_{qr}^{\,\pm}\widetilde{t}_{rp}^{\,\pm}~,

(41)

\displaystyle\zeta_{pqr}^{\,\mr{R}}:=\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{R}}\widetilde{t}_{qr}^{\,\mr{R}}\widetilde{t}_{rp}^{\,\mr{R}}~,

\displaystyle\zeta_{pqr}^{\,\mr{f}}:=\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{f}}\widetilde{t}_{qr}^{\,\mr{f}}\widetilde{t}_{rp}^{\,\mr{f}}~,

\displaystyle\zeta_{pqr}^{g}:=\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,g}\widetilde{t}_{qr}^{\,g}\widetilde{t}_{rp}^{\,g}~.

Then the cocycle condition (
37
) is equivalent to

\boxed{\big(\zeta_{pqr}^{+},\zeta_{pqr}^{-},\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{R}},\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{f}},\zeta_{pqr}^{g}\big)\in C^{\mr{phys}}~.}

(42)

From (
29
),

\zeta_{pqr}^{-}=\zeta_{pqr}^{+}=\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{R}}~.

(43)

We will also define
\zeta_{pqr}^{s}:=\zeta_{pqr}^{-}=\zeta_{pqr}^{+}
for the cocycle corresponding to the
\mr{Spin(4)}
factor of
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}}
.
To derive cohomological conditions from (
42
), we need to identify what each cocycle represents. By definition,

t_{pq}^{g}:=\left[\,\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,g}\right]:U_{pq}\longrightarrow\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}/C^{\mr{grb}}\cong G^{\mr{gauge}}~,

(44)

represents a choice of transition functions on the gauge bundle
P^{\mr{gauge}}\to{\mathbb{X}}
. The cocycle
\zeta_{pqr}^{g}
represents the ’t Hooft flux of the gauge bundle, i.e., the obstruction to lifting the structure group from
G^{\mr{gauge}}\cong\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}/C^{\mr{grb}}
to
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}
:

\mu(b):=\left[\zeta_{pqr}^{g}\right]\in H^{2}\big({\mathbb{X}},C^{\mr{grb}}\big)~.

(45)

The requirement just below (
29
) implies that
\zeta_{pqr}^{s}
represents the second Stiefel-Whitney class of
T{\mathbb{X}}
:

w_{2}({\mathbb{X}}):=w_{2}(T{\mathbb{X}})=\left[\zeta_{pqr}^{s}\right]\in H^{2}\left({\mathbb{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{2}\right)~,

(46)

which is the obstruction to
{\mathbb{X}}
being spin. Similarly,
\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{R}}
represents the obstruction to lifting the structure group of the
\mr{R}
-symmetry bundle
P^{\mr{R}}\to{\mathbb{X}}
from
\mr{SO(3)_{R}}
to
\mr{SU(2)_{R}}
:

w_{2}\big(P^{\mr{R}}\big):=\big[\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{R}}\big]\in H^{2}\left({\mathbb{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{2}\right)~.

(47)

From (
43
), we immediately get the first cohomological condition, namely

\boxed{w_{2}(P^{\mr{R}})=w_{2}({\mathbb{X}})~.}

(48)

Whenever we have hypermultiplets in the theory, the condition (
42
)
can be written using item
2
above as

\displaystyle\rho\left(\zeta_{pqr}^{g}\right)\zeta_{pqr}^{s}\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{f}}=1\iff\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{f}}=\rho\left(\zeta_{pqr}^{g}\right)^{-1}\zeta_{pqr}^{s}\in{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}~.

(49)

Note that (
49
) is equivalent to (
42
) when
C^{\mr{phys}}
is the maximal admissible subgroup.
21
21

21

We emphasize that the condition (
49
) is
not
equivalent to (
42
) when
C^{\mr{phys}}
is
not
the maximal admissible subgroup.
The simplest counterexample is when
Z(\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}})
is a cyclic group with generator
g
. Consider

C^{\mr{phys}}=\langle(-1,-1,-1,-\rho(g)^{-1},g)\rangle~.

In this case,
C^{\mr{phys}}
is not the maximal admissible subgroup, and clearly,

p_{4}(C^{\mr{phys}})\cong Z(\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}})~,\quad(p_{1}\times p_{2})(C^{\mr{phys}})\cong\mr{diag}(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{3})~.

Now, if
g
is not a square in
Z(\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}})
, the element
(1,1,1,\rho(g)^{-1},g)\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{3}\times Z(G^{\mr{f}})\times Z(\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}})
satisfies (
49
) but is not an element of
C^{\mr{phys}}
. More generally, whenever
C^{\mr{phys}}
is a proper subgroup of the maximal admissible subgroup, (
49
) is not equivalent to (
42
).

We can turn the condition (
49
)
into a cohomological restriction on the background fields as follows.
Recall that we are choosing generic masses so we are reducing the flavor symmetry
to its maximal torus.
Note that as a representation of
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}
over
\mathbb{C}
, there is an isotypical decomposition
(here we
choose
a complex structure on
{\cal R}
):

\rho\cong\bigoplus_{u}\rho_{u}~,

(50)

where each summand
\rho_{u}
is an irreducible representation.
Also, note that the irreducible representations for
u\not=u^{\prime}
might be isomorphic.
Choose an isomorphism:

{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}\cong\prod_{u}\mr{U(1)}_{u}~.

(51)

We let
p_{4,u}
denote the projection of
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}}
to the
u^{\mathrm{th}}
component of (
51
).

Consider any irreducible subrepresentation
\rho_{u}
of
\rho
.
Since
C^{\mr{grb}}
is finite Abelian, Schur’s Lemma
22
22

22

We are working over
\mathbb{C}
.

implies that the image

\rho_{u}(C^{\mr{grb}})
in
\mr{Aut}(\rho_{u})
is
always a cyclic group acting by scalars.
This image, together with
-1
, generates a cyclic subgroup
{\cal Z}_{u}\subset\mr{U(1)}
. Note there is a canonical injective homomorphism
\rho_{s}:{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\cal Z}_{u}
. Moreover, the condition (
31
) implies that there is an inclusion

\iota:p_{3,u}(C^{\mr{phys}})\hookrightarrow{\cal Z}_{u}
and hence
p_{3,u}(C^{\mr{phys}})
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{Z}}/n_{u}{\mathbb{Z}}
, thus defining a set of integers
\{n_{u}\}
.

Next,
(p_{3,u})_{*}(P^{\mr{phys}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{phys}})=(P^{\mr{f},u},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{f},u})
is a well-defined principal
\mr{U(1)}/p_{3,u}(C^{\mr{phys}})
-bundle over
{\mathbb{X}}
with connection. We should think of
\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{f},u}
as the “background gauge fields for the
flavor symmetry.” Let
c_{1}(u)\in H^{2}({\mathbb{X}},{\mathbb{Z}})
be the first Chern class of the
\mr{U}(1)
-bundle obtained by transfer of structure group of
P^{\mr{f},u}
along the isomorphism
23
23

23

Note that this isomorphism is
not
the
n_{u}^{th}
-power map. More precisely, it is the usual isomorphism obtained from the short exact sequence

1\longrightarrow p_{3,u}(C^{\mr{phys}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mr{U(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P_{n_{u}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mr{U(1)}\longrightarrow 1~,

where
\iota
is the inclusion map and
P_{n_{u}}
is the
n_{u}^{th}
-power map.

\mr{U(1)}/p_{3,u}(C^{\mr{phys}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mr{U}(1)~.

(52)

Let
r_{n}:{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}
denote reduction modulo
n
. The cocycle
[\zeta^{\mr{f}}_{pqr}]
is then a representative of
r_{n_{u}}(c_{1}(u))
and is the obstruction to lifting
P^{\mr{f},u}
to
a
\mr{U(1)}
-bundle along the quotient map
\mr{U(1)}\to\mr{U(1)}/p_{3,u}(C^{\mr{phys}})
. The existence of
P^{\mr{phys}}
implies the following cohomological restriction on background fields
(‘‘background 1-form symmetry connections’’):
24
24

24

The second term in (
53
) is the image of
w_{2}({\mathbb{X}})
under the map
\rho_{s}:H^{2}({\mathbb{X}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})\to H^{2}({\mathbb{X}},{\cal Z}_{u})
induced by the canonical homomorphism
\rho_{s}:{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\cal Z}_{u}
.

\boxed{\rho_{u}(\mu(b))+\rho_{s}(w_{2}({\mathbb{X}}))+\iota\circ r_{n_{u}}(c_{1}(u))=0\in H^{2}({\mathbb{X}},{\cal Z}_{u})}~,

(53)

which must hold for all the irreducible subrepresentations of
\rho
.

We now show that (
53
) is equivalent to the existence of
P^{\mr{phys}}
when

C^{\mr{phys}}
is the maximal admissible subgroup and the flavor symmetry is
G^{\mr{f}}=\mr{U(1)}
.
In the case that
G^{\mr{f}}=\mr{U(1)}^{d}
, we simply take a fiber product.

Consider now a principal
(\widetilde{G}/C^{\mr{grb}})
-bundle
P^{\mr{gauge}}
with ’t Hooft flux
\mu(b)\in H^{2}({\mathbb{X}},C^{\mr{grb}})
, a
\mr{R}
-symmetry bundle
P^{\mr{R}}
which is an
\mr{SO(3)_{R}}
-bundle with second Stiefel-Whitney class
w_{2}(P^{\mr{R}})\in H^{2}\left({\mathbb{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{2}\right)
and a flavor symmetry bundle
P^{\mr{f}}
which is a
\mr{U(1)}/p_{3}(C^{\mr{phys}})
-bundle. The condition (
31
) implies that
p_{3}(C^{\mr{phys}})\hookrightarrow{\cal Z}\subset\mr{U(1)}
where
{\cal Z}
is a subgroup generated by
\rho(C^{\mr{grb}})
and
-1
. There is an isomorphism,

p_{3}(C^{\mr{phys}})\cong{\mathbb{Z}}/n{\mathbb{Z}}~,

(54)

for some
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
. We also have the frame bundle
\mr{Fr}({\mathbb{X}})
which is an
\mr{SO(4)}
-bundle with second Stiefel-Whitney class
w_{2}({\mathbb{X}})\in H^{2}\left({\mathbb{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{2}\right)
. Moreover, assume that the cohomological conditions (
48
) and (
53
) are satisfied. We now show that these bundles can be assembled into a
G^{\mr{phys}}
-bundle
P^{\mr{phys}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{X}}
. Indeed, take transition functions

\displaystyle t_{pq}^{s}:U_{pq}\longrightarrow\mr{SO(4)}~,

(55)

\displaystyle t_{pq}^{\mr{R}}:U_{pq}\longrightarrow\mr{SO(3)_{R}}~,

\displaystyle t_{pq}^{\mr{f}}:U_{pq}\longrightarrow\mr{U(1)}/p_{3}(C^{\mr{phys}})~,

\displaystyle t_{pq}^{g}:U_{pq}\longrightarrow\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}/C^{\mr{grb}}~,

on
P^{\mr{gauge}},\mr{Fr}({\mathbb{X}}),P^{\mr{R}}
and
P^{\mr{f}}
respectively, and consider local lifts – as defined in (
38
) – to

\begin{split}\left(\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,+},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,-}\right):U_{pq}&\longrightarrow\mr{Spin(4)}\cong\mr{SU(2)}\times\mr{SU(2)}~,\\
\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{R}}:U_{pq}&\longrightarrow\mr{SU(2)_{R}}~,\\
\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{f}}:U_{pq}&\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}~,\\
\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,g}:U_{pq}&\longrightarrow\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}~,\end{split}

(56)

where the lift
\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{f}}
is along the homomorphism

\displaystyle\pi:{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mr{U(1)}/p_{3}(C^{\mr{phys}})

(57)

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\longmapsto\left[\exp\left(\frac{2\pi\mathsf{i}x}{n}\right)\right]~,

where
n
is given by (
54
).
Thus

\left[\exp\left(\frac{2\pi\mathsf{i}\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{f}}}{n}\right)\right]=t_{pq}^{\mr{f}}~.

(58)

The first Chern class
c_{1}(\mr{f})\in H^{2}({\mathbb{X}},{\mathbb{Z}})
of the
\mr{U}(1)
-bundle obtained by transfer of structure group of
P^{\mr{f}}
along the isomorphism
\mr{U(1)}/p_{3}(C^{\mr{phys}})\cong\mr{U}(1)
(see footnote
23
) is represented by the cocycle

\eta_{pqr}^{\mr{f}}:=\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{f}}+\widetilde{t}_{qr}^{\,\mr{f}}+\widetilde{t}_{rp}^{\,\mr{f}}:U_{pqr}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}~.

(59)

Also, define

\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{f}}:=\exp\left(\frac{2\pi\mathsf{i}\eta_{pqr}^{\mr{f}}}{n}\right),

(60)

which is the cocycle representing the
\mathsf{\,mod\,}~n
reduction of
c_{1}(\mr{f})
. The cocycles for
\left(\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,-},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,+}\right),\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,\mr{R}}
and
\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,g}
are denoted by

\displaystyle\left(\zeta_{pqr}^{+},\zeta_{pqr}^{-}\right):U_{pqr}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mr{diag}(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2})~,

(61)

\displaystyle\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{R}}:U_{pqr}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{2}~,

\displaystyle\zeta_{pqr}^{g}:U_{pqr}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow C^{\mr{grb}}=p_{4}(C^{\mr{phys}})~,

respectively.
The condition (
48
) implies that, perhaps after a change of local trivialization,
\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{R}}=\zeta_{pqr}^{\pm}=\zeta_{pqr}^{s}
.
If we take

t_{pq}^{\mr{phys}}=\left[\left(\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,+},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,-},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\mr{R}},t_{pq}^{\mr{f}},\widetilde{t}_{pq}^{\,g}\right)\right]~,

(62)

it follows that

t_{pq}^{\mr{phys}}t_{qr}^{\mr{phys}}t_{rp}^{\mr{phys}}=\left[\left(\zeta_{pqr}^{+},\zeta_{pqr}^{-},\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{R}},\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{f}},\zeta_{pqr}^{g}\right)\right]~.

(63)

Note that
\zeta_{pqr}^{g}
is valued in
C^{\mr{grb}}
and
\left(\zeta_{pqr}^{-},\zeta_{pqr}^{+},\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{R}}\right)
is valued in
\left(p_{1}\times p_{2}\right)\left(C^{\mr{phys}}\right)
and (
53
) implies that

\rho\left(\zeta_{pqr}^{g}\right)\zeta_{pqr}^{s}\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{f}}=1~.

(64)

As noted above, for maximal admissible subgroup
C^{\mr{phys}}
, this is equivalent to the condition

\left(\zeta_{pqr}^{+},\zeta_{pqr}^{-},\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{R}},\zeta_{pqr}^{\mr{f}},\zeta_{pqr}^{g}\right)\in C^{\mr{phys}}~.

(65)

Thus
t_{pq}^{\mr{phys}}
satisfies the cocycle condition for
G^{\mr{phys}}
and hence can be used to construct a principal
G^{\mr{phys}}
-bundle
P^{\mr{phys}}
.

We now show that in specific examples, (
53
) reduces to familiar conditions known from the literature.

1. Pure
\mathcal{N}=2
Super Yang-Mills (SYM) Theory with gauge group
G^{\mr{gauge}}
:

In this case,
C^{\mr{phys}}
is constrained to be of the form

C^{\mr{phys}}=\mr{diag}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{3}\right)\times Z~,

(66)

where
Z
is some subgroup of
Z(\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}})
. The only condition that we get from the existence of
P^{\mr{phys}}
is that

w_{2}({\mathbb{X}})=w_{2}(P^{\mr{R}})~.

(67)

2.
\mr{SU(2)}

{\cal N}=2
SYM with fundamental flavors:

For fundamental hypermultiplets,

\rho_{u}(\mu(b))=:w_{2}(P^{\mr{gauge}})\in H^{2}({\mathbb{X}},\mathbb{Z}_{2})~.

(68)

If we choose not to “turn on the flavor bundle” so that
c_{1}\left(u\right)=0
, (
53
) yields
25
25

25

Note that we get the same condition if the physical theory corresponds to the choice

C^{\mr{phys}}\cong\langle(-1,-1,-1,1,-1)\rangle~.

Indeed, in this case (
49
) implies that
\rho\left(\zeta_{pqr}^{g}\right)=\zeta_{pqr}^{s}
which implies
w_{2}(P^{\mr{gauge}})=w_{2}({\mathbb{X}})
.

w_{2}(P^{\mr{gauge}})=w_{2}({\mathbb{X}})=w_{2}(P^{\mr{R}})~,

(69)

which was already noted in
MW (97)
for the twisted theory. If background
\mr{U(1)}
flavor gauge bundles are turned on, (
53
) reduces to

c_{1}(u)\equiv w_{2}({\mathbb{X}})+w_{2}(P^{\mr{gauge}})\,\,\,\mr{mod\,}2~,

(70)

which is precisely the condition proposed in
AFM (22)
. (The condition (
67
) still applies.)
We stress again that – contrary to the viewpoint found in

AFM (22)
;
MW (97)
– these are conditions for the physical theory,
not just specific to topologically twisted theories.

3.
\mathcal{N}=2^{*}
Theory:

Matter in the
\mathcal{N}=2^{*}
theory consists of a massive adjoint hypermultiplet. Since the center of the gauge group acts trivially on the adjoint representation, it follows that

\rho_{u}(\mu(b))=0~.

(71)

Thus, (
53
) yields

c_{1}(u)\equiv w_{2}({\mathbb{X}})\,\,\,\mr{mod\,}2~.

(72)

Therefore,
c_{1}(u)
can be identified with the characteristic class of a spin
{}^{\mr{c}}
-structure
26
26

26

Recall that a spin
{}^{\mr{c}}
structure on
\mr{Fr}({\mathbb{X}})
is a reduction of structure group of
\mr{Fr}({\mathbb{X}})
from
\mr{SO(4)}
to
\mr{Spin^{c}(4)}=(\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{U(1)})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
along the homomorphism
\mr{Spin^{c}(4)}\to\mr{Spin(4)}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\cong\mr{SO(4)}
.

on
{\mathbb{X}}
. This condition was obtained in
LMn (97)
;
MM (21)
. (The condition (
67
) still applies.)

4.
d=4
,
\mathcal{N}=4
Super Yang-Mills Theory:

Consider the 4d,
{\cal N}=4
super Yang-Mills theory. The field content consists of an
{\cal N}=2
vectormultiplet and a massless adjoint hypermultiplet. The theory has an
\mr{SU(4)_{R}~R}
-symmetry. So,

\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}}=\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{SU(4)_{R}}\times\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}~,

(73)

and now
C^{\mr{phys}}
is a subgroup of the center of
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}}
, which is

Z(\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}})=\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times Z(\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}})~.

(74)

The structure group of the physical theory is
G^{\mr{phys}}=\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}}/C^{\mr{phys}}
. The gauge, frame, and
\mr{R}
-symmetry bundles can be obtained via pushforward by the pertinent projection maps as in the general discussion. We also have the identification (
34
).

The condition that the fields of the theory be singlets under
C^{\mr{phys}}
along with (
33
) implies that
C^{\mr{phys}}
be a subgroup of the maximal admissible group defined by
27
27

27

The fields in the
\mathcal{N}=4
vectormultiplet are: a
\widetilde{G}
-connection, an
\mathsf{Ad}(\widetilde{G})
-valued Weyl fermion in the
4
of
\mr{SU(4)_{R}}
and an
\mathsf{Ad}(\widetilde{G})
-valued scalar in the
6
of
\mr{SU(4)_{R}}
. Thus, the invariance of the Weyl fermion under
C^{\mr{phys}}
dictates the form of
C_{\mr{max}}^{\mr{phys}}
. Scalars are automatically singlets under
C_{\mr{max}}^{\mr{phys}}
.

C_{\mr{max}}^{\mr{phys}}:=\{(\zeta,\zeta,\zeta,g)\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times Z(\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}})\}~,

(75)

where in the last component,
\zeta
is understood to be the image of the inclusion
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{4}
. Let us now derive the cohomological condition from the existence of the
G^{\mr{phys}}
-bundle
P^{\mr{phys}}
. Note that the structure group of the
\mr{R}
-symmetry bundle
P^{\mr{R}}
is
\mr{SU(4)_{R}}/p_{3}(C^{\mr{phys}})\cong\mr{SU(4)_{R}}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Thus there is an associated obstruction
w_{2}(P^{\mr{R}})\in H^{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Z}_{2})
to lifting this bundle to an
\mr{SU(4)_{R}}
-bundle. Following the same procedure as above, we find the cohomological condition

w_{2}({\mathbb{X}})=w_{2}(P^{\mr{R}})~.

(76)

In Section
6.1
, we will see that this theory admits three different twistings associated with three pairs
\left(G_{i}^{\mr{tw}},\varphi_{i}^{\mr{tw}}:G_{i}^{\mr{tw}}\longrightarrow G^{\mr{phys}}\right)
of twisting homomorphisms.

5
Dynamical And Background Fields In Lagrangian Theories

In order to define a quantum theory we need to choose a set of background fields
{\cal F}^{\mr{bck}}
and a fibration

\pi:{\cal F}^{\mr{phys}}\to{\cal F}^{\mr{bck}}~.

(77)

The map
\pi
is not necessarily surjective. In principle, some background fields cannot be consistently coupled to the quantum theory. However, for simplicity, we will write subsequent formulae as if
\pi
were surjective.
The
dynamical fields
are the fields in the fiber of
\pi
. Using the exponentiated action
e^{-S}
to define a (formal) measure
we (formally) integrate over the dynamical fields to produce functions of the
background fields. Thus the process of path integration defines a pushforward so that the partition function

\mr{Z}:=(\pi)_{*}(e^{-S})
defines a function on the space of background fields:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal F}^{\mr{phys}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal F}^{\mr{bck}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}~.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{-S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}

(78)

Note that the process of path integration usually introduces a scale
\Lambda
. We can regard
\Lambda
as one of the background fields, and if we wish to emphasize the scale dependence we write
(\pi_{\Lambda})_{*}
for integration above background fields with fixed
\Lambda
.
In the superconformal case,
\Lambda
can be replaced by the ultraviolet coupling
\tau_{\mr{uv}}
which is also a background field, and the path integral will depend on
\tau_{\mr{uv}}
.

Now we choose our set of background fields to be

{\cal F}^{\mr{bck}}:=\{\Lambda,m,(P^{\mr{bck}}\to{\mathbb{X}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{bck}}),b,\Phi^{\mr{grav}}\}~,

(79)

where

(P^{\mr{bck}}\to{\mathbb{X}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{bck}}):=(p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3})_{*}(P^{\mr{phys}}\to{\mathbb{X}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{phys}})~,

(80)

is a principal bundle with connection over
{\mathbb{X}}
with structure group

G^{\mr{bck}}:=\big(\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{SU(2)}_{\mr{R}}\times G^{\mr{f}}\big)/C^{\mr{bck}}=(p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3})(G^{\mr{phys}})~.

(81)

Note that
C^{\mr{bck}}
is a subgroup of the center of
\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{SU(2)}_{\mr{R}}\times G^{\mr{f}}

and note that the analog of (
34
) also holds for
{\cal F}^{\mr{bck}}
.
We might or might not wish to consider
some subset of the gerbes
b
to be dynamical.
28
28

28

If one adopts the quiche picture
FMT (22)
;
Fre (22)
then the
five
-dimensional field
b
is always dynamical. Moreover, the isomorphism to the gauge theory gerbe is likewise dynamical. The distinction we discuss in this paragraph is then phrased in terms of choices one makes on the topological boundary.

The choice of which subgroup of gerbes
is dynamical leads to different gauge theories for different gauge groups
G^{\mr{gauge}}\cong\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}/C^{\mr{grb}}
with the
same simply connected cover
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}
. For simplicity, we will consider
b
to be nondynamical.
In general, the fibration
\pi
is not a direct product because the fiber of dynamical fields depends on the background fields. For example, the set of dynamical gauge fields depends on the
background gerbe
b
. Moreover, there are consistency conditions on the background fields. See, for example, (
53
) above. See also Remark
2
.

It follows from the above discussion that the set of dynamical fields includes
\Phi^{\mr{phys}}

along with the connection
on the
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}/C^{\mr{grb}}
gauge bundle with connection obtained by transfer of structure group
associated with the projection homomorphism
p_{1}
.

In section
6
we will
explicitly describe one topological twisting of an arbitrary Lagrangian
d=4

{\cal N}=2
theory (with generic masses) using a transfer of structure group based on a homomorphism
\varphi^{\mr{tw}}:G^{\mr{tw}}\to G^{\mr{phys}}
, where

G^{\mr{tw}}:=\big(\mr{Spin(4)}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}\times\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}\big)/C^{\mr{tw}}~,

(82)

where
C^{\mr{tw}}
is a subgroup of the center of
\mr{Spin(4)}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}\times\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}
.
The key data that must be specified are the Abelian group
C^{\mr{tw}}
and
the homomorphism
\varphi^{\mr{tw}}
. We will take
C^{\mr{tw}}
to be a finite group. Moreover, we will require that the projection of

C^{\mr{tw}}
to
Z(\mr{Spin(4)})
surjects to
\langle(-1,-1)\rangle\cong{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
so that we can have equations (
84
) and
(
19
) below. For simplicity, we restrict the discussion to the case where
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}
is a simple group.

The fields of the twisted theory are in part determined by the bundle with connection
(P^{\mr{tw}}\to{\mathbb{X}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{tw}})

for structure group
G^{\mr{tw}}
, (which must satisfy a condition analogous to (
34
)).
We can write the fields of the twisted theory informally as:

{\cal F}^{\mr{tw}}:=\{\tau,m,(P^{\mr{tw}}\to{\mathbb{X}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{tw}}),\Phi^{\mr{tw}},b\}~,

(83)

where
\Phi^{\mr{tw}}\in\Gamma(E^{\mr{tw}})
and
E^{\mr{tw}}\to{\mathbb{X}}
is a suitable bundle associated to
P^{\mr{tw}}\to{\mathbb{X}}
,
and

\varphi^{\mr{tw}}_{*}(P^{\mr{tw}}\to{\mathbb{X}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{tw}},\Phi^{\mr{tw}})=(P^{\mr{phys}}\to{\mathbb{X}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{phys}},\Phi^{\mr{phys}})~.

(84)

The choice of
G^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}
and
\varphi^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}
is such that there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal F}^{\mr{tw}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal F}^{\mr{phys}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal F}^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cal F}^{\mr{bck}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}~,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{*}^{\mr{tw}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{-S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{*}^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mr{Z}}

(85)

where

\displaystyle G^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}

\displaystyle:=(\mr{Spin(4)}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}})/C^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}~,

(86)

\displaystyle{\cal F}^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}

\displaystyle:=\{m,(P^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}),b\}~,

(87)

has the property that
\mr{Z}\circ\varphi_{*}^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}
is locally constant as a function of

(P^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}})
. Again, given the key choices of
C^{\mr{tw}}
and

\varphi^{\mr{tw}}
, the finite Abelian group
C^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}
and the homomorphism

\varphi_{*}^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}
are determined by the integration process associated to
\pi_{\Lambda}
.
Once again, we note that in the superconformal case,
{\cal F}^{\mr{tw},\mr{bck}}
also includes the ultraviolet coupling, and the topologically twisted partition function will depend on it.

Imposing once again equation (
34
) we see that the partition function of the topologically twisted theory again depends on the generalized spin
{}^{\mr}{c}
structure, and no other background field data, aside from the diffeomorphism type of
{\mathbb{X}}
and the ’t Hooft fluxes.

6
Twisting General Renormalizable Lagrangian 4d
{\cal N}=2
Theories

In this section, we will describe the choices
C^{\mr{tw}}
and
\varphi^{\mr{tw}}

for a general
d=4

{\cal N}=2
gauge theory with a simple gauge group, which give us a topologically twisted version of the theory.

In order to construct the structure group
G^{\mr{tw}}
of a topologically twisted theory
we begin by defining
H:=p_{4}(C^{\mr{phys}})\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
and we construct a homomorphism

\varphi_{\rho}^{G}:H\to{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}~,

(88)

where
{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}
is a maximal torus of the flavor group. To construct it,
we start with the isotypical decomposition of
\rho
, as in Section
4
,

\rho\cong\bigoplus_{u}\rho_{u}~,

(89)

which gives us an isomorphism

{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}\cong\prod_{u}\mr{U(1)}_{u}~.

(90)

As in Section
4
, for each irreducible representation
\rho_{u}
,

\langle\rho_{u}(p_{4}(C^{\mr{phys}})),-1\rangle\subset{\rm Aut}(\rho_{u})~,

(91)

is a finite cyclic group isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}/{n_{u}}\mathbb{Z}
for some positive integer
n_{u}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
.
We consider this cyclic group to be the standard cyclic group of roots of unity in
\mr{U(1)}
. Then we define

\varphi_{\rho}^{G}(g,\zeta):=\prod_{u}\left(\rho_{u}(g^{-1})\zeta\right)~.

(92)

Now define

\widetilde{G}^{\mr{tw}}:=\mr{Spin(4)}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}\times\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}~.

(93)

Writing a typical element of
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{tw}}
as
((u_{1},u_{2}),\mu,g)
, where
(u_{1},u_{2})\in\mr{Spin(4)}\cong\mr{SU(2)}\times\mr{SU(2)}
,
\mu\in{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}
and
g\in\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}
, there is a crucial homomorphism based on the Witten homomorphism:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varphi:\widetilde{G}^{\mr{tw}}&\longrightarrow\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}}~,\\
\varphi((u_{1},u_{2}),\mu,g)&:=((u_{1},u_{2}),u_{2},\mu,g)~,\end{split}

(94)

Now, define an injective homomorphism

\displaystyle\psi^{\mr{tw}}:H

\displaystyle\longrightarrow Z(\widetilde{G}^{\mr{tw}})

(95)

\displaystyle(g,\zeta)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\psi^{\mr{tw}}(g,\zeta):=(\zeta,\zeta,\varphi_{\rho}^{G}(g,\zeta),g)~,

and observe that

\varphi(\psi^{\mr{tw}}(H))\subset C^{\mr{phys}}~,

(96)

when
C^{\mr{phys}}
is the maximal admissible subgroup.
In the notation of Section
3
, we can take

C^{\mr{tw}}=\psi^{\mr{tw}}(H)
when
C^{\mr{phys}}
is the maximal admissible subgroup. In general, we will take:

C^{\mr{tw}}=\left\{(\zeta,\zeta,\mu,g):(\zeta,\zeta,\zeta,\mu,g)\in C^{\mr{phys}}\right\}~.

(97)

In any case,
\varphi
descends to a homomorphism

\overline{\varphi}:G^{\mr{tw}}:=\widetilde{G}^{\mr{tw}}/\psi^{\mr{tw}}(H)\longrightarrow G^{\mr{phys}}=\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}}/C^{\mr{phys}}~.

(98)

and in the notation of Section
3
,

\varphi^{\mr{tw}}=\overline{\varphi}~.

(99)

Using the condition (
53
), one can show that the transition functions

\displaystyle t_{pq}^{\mr{tw}}:=[((\widetilde{t}^{+}_{pq},\widetilde{t}^{-}_{pq}),\widetilde{t}^{\,\mr{f}}_{pq},\widetilde{t}^{\,g}_{pq})]:U_{pq}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow G^{\mr{tw}}=\frac{\mr{Spin(4)}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}\times\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}}{\psi^{\mr{tw}}(H)}~,

(100)

where the various components are the components of
t^{\mr{phys}}_{pq}
as in (
39
), satisfy the cocycle condition. Thus we can construct a principal
G^{\mr{tw}}
-bundle
P^{\mr{tw}}
with transition functions
t_{pq}^{\mr{tw}}
. The physical bundle

P^{\mr{phys}}
with connection is then obtained by transfer of structure group from the
P^{\mr{tw}}
bundle with connection under the map induced by the group homomorphism
\overline{\varphi}:G^{\mr{tw}}\to G^{\mr{phys}}
.

6.1
Three Inequivalent Twistings of 4d
{\cal N}=4
Super Yang-Mills Theory

In this section, we describe the three inequivalent twistings of 4d,
{\cal N}=4
super Yang-Mills theory discussed in
Yam (88)
;
VW (94)
;
Mar (95)
;
DPW (20)
, using the transfer of structure group. The three twisting homomorphisms are based on the three homomorphisms
\mr{Spin(4)}\rightarrow\mr{SU(4)}
discussed in
VW (94)
:

1.

The first homomorphism
\mr{Spin(4)}\rightarrow\mr{SU(4)}
is chosen such that the
4
of
\mr{SU(4)}
decomposes to
(\textbf{2},\textbf{1})\oplus(\textbf{1},\textbf{2})
under pullback. This corresponds to the Geometric Langlands (
GL
) twist
KW (06)
, originally discussed in
Yam (88)
;
Mar (95)
.

2.

The second homomorphism
\mr{Spin(4)}\rightarrow\mr{SU(4)}
is chosen such that the
4
of
\mr{SU(4)}
decomposes to
(\textbf{1},\textbf{2})\oplus(\textbf{1},\textbf{1})\oplus(\textbf{1},\textbf{1})
under pullback. This corresponds to the usual Donaldson-Witten (
DW
) twist, thinking of the
{\cal N}=4
theory as an
{\cal N}=2
theory
Wit (88)
.

3.

The third homomorphism
\mr{Spin(4)}\rightarrow\mr{SU(4)}
is chosen such that the
4
of
\mr{SU(4)}
decomposes to
(\textbf{1},\textbf{2})\oplus(\textbf{1},\textbf{2})
under pullback. This corresponds to the Vafa-Witten (
VW
) twist
VW (94)
.

We now describe the twisting group
G^{\mr{tw}}
and twisting homomorphisms
\varphi^{\mr{tw}}:G^{\mr{tw}}\to G^{\mr{phys}}
in each case. The notation in this section follows Example
4
above.

1.

Define

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{1}^{\mr{tw}}=\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{U(1)_{R}}\times\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}

(101)

\displaystyle C_{1}^{\mr{tw}}=\{(\zeta,\zeta,1,g):(\zeta,\zeta,\zeta,g)\in C^{\mr{phys}}\}~.

The twisting group is
G_{1}^{\mr{tw}}=\widetilde{G}_{1}^{\mr{tw}}/C_{1}^{\mr{tw}}
. The twisting homomorphism is given by

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}^{\mr{GL}}:\widetilde{G}_{1}^{\mr{tw}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}}

(102)

\displaystyle\big(\left(u_{1},u_{2}\right),e^{i\theta},g\big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\big(\left(u_{1},u_{2}\right),\mr{diag}\big(e^{i\theta}u_{1},e^{-i\theta}u_{2}\big),g\big)~,

where
\left(u_{1},u_{2}\right)\in\mr{SU(2)}\times\mr{SU(2)}\cong\mr{Spin(4)}.
Then
\varphi_{1}^{\mr{GL}}
descends to a homomorphism

\overline{\varphi}_{1}^{\mr{GL}}:G_{1}^{\mr{tw}}\longrightarrow G^{\mr{phys}}~.

(103)

The
\mr{U(1)_{R}}
factor in
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{tw}}
reflects the fact that a
\mr{U(1)}
subgroup of
\mr{SU(4)}
commutes with the image of the first homomorphism
\mr{Spin(4)}\longrightarrow\mr{SU(4)}
and hence is a residual
\mr{R}
-symmetry of the twisted theory.

2.

Define

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{2}^{\mr{tw}}=\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{U(2)_{R}}\times\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}~,

(104)

\displaystyle C_{2}^{\mr{tw}}=\left\{(a,a,1,g):(a,a,a,g)\in C^{\mr{phys}}\right\}~.

The twisting group is
G_{2}^{\mr{tw}}=\widetilde{G}_{2}^{\mr{tw}}/C_{2}^{\mr{tw}}
. The homomorphism is given by

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}^{\mr{DW}}:\widetilde{G}_{2}^{\mr{tw}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}}

(105)

\displaystyle\big(\big(u_{1},u_{2}\big),u,g\big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\big(\big(u_{1},u_{2}\big),\mr{diag}\big(\tfrac{u_{2}}{\sqrt{\mr{det}u}},u\big),g\big)~.

Then
\varphi_{2}^{\mr{DW}}
descends to a homomorphism

\overline{\varphi}_{2}^{\mr{DW}}:G_{2}^{\mr{tw}}\longrightarrow G^{\mr{phys}}~.

(106)

The
\mr{U(2)_{R}}
factor in
\widetilde{G}^{\mr{tw}}
reflects residual
\mr{R}
-symmetry of the twisted theory.

3.

Define

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{3}^{\mr{tw}}=\mr{Spin(4)}\times\mr{SU(2)}_{R}\times\widetilde{G}^{\mr{gauge}}~,

(107)

\displaystyle C_{3}^{\mr{tw}}=\left\{(\zeta,\zeta,1,g):(\zeta,\zeta,\zeta,g)\in C^{\mr{phys}}\right\}~.

The twisting group is
G_{3}^{\mr{tw}}=\widetilde{G}_{3}^{\mr{tw}}/C_{3}^{\mr{tw}}
. To define the twisting homomorphism, introduce the map

\displaystyle\begin{split}f:\mr{SU(2)}\times\mr{SU(2)_{R}}&\longrightarrow\mr{SU(4)_{R}}\\
\left(u,\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha&\beta\\
-\bar{\beta}&\bar{\alpha}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)&\longmapsto\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha u&\beta u\\
\bar{\beta}u&\bar{\alpha}u\end{smallmatrix}\right)~.\end{split}

(108)

It is easy to check that
f
is a well-defined homomorphism. We define the twisting homomorphism
by

\displaystyle\varphi_{3}^{\mr{VW}}:\widetilde{G}_{3}^{\mr{tw}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\widetilde{G}^{\mr{phys}}

(109)

\displaystyle\left(\left(u_{1},u_{2}\right),u,g\right)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\left(\left(u_{1},u_{2}\right),f\left(u_{2},u\right),g\right)~.

Clearly,
\varphi_{3}^{\mr{VW}}
descends to a homomorphism

\overline{\varphi}_{3}^{\mr{VW}}:G_{3}^{\mr{tw}}\longrightarrow G^{\mr{phys}}~.

(110)

Again, the twisted theory has residual
\mr{SU(2)_{R}~R}
-symmetry.

7
Independence Of Topological Correlators Under Continuous Deformation Of Background Connection

In equation (
18
) et. seq. we gave a general argument that
backgrounds defined via transfer of structure group satisfying (
18
) will have partition functions that are invariant under
continuous deformations of the background connections. In this section, we review the more traditional argument that applies to Lagrangian field theories. We begin by spelling out the sublocus of topologically twisted fields and the twisted action in more detail.

The rules of
d=4

{\cal N}=2
supersymmetry dictate that
\Phi^{\mr{phys}}
be a section of the direct sum of
G^{\mr{phys}}
-vector bundles associated to the representations listed in Tables

and
2
.

\rho

\overline{\varrho}

\varrho
. Various matter fields in Table

). The fields

\mf{su(2)}_{\mr{R}}
-doublet scalars.
In particular,
M_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}
and
\widetilde{M}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}
are related by complex conjugation.

\Phi^{\mr{tw}}
. Finally there is a

-connection

on

satisfying the requirement analogous to (

).

P^{\mr{tw}}
to
P^{\mr{phys}}
along the homomorphism
\varphi^{\mr{tw}}:G^{\mr{tw}}\to G^{\mr{phys}}

then determines the connection
\varphi^{\mr{tw}}_{*}(\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{tw}})
on
P^{\mr{phys}}
.

). The connections that enter the action are given by pushforwards of

for the definition of various bundles. Here

Note that the requirement analogous to (
34
) for
(P^{\mr{tw}},\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{tw}})

forces the connection
\omega_{\mu}
on
\mr{Fr}({\mathbb{X}})
to be the LC connection

and the twisting homomorphism

forces the connection on the

to be the SD part

of the LC connection.

The supercharges are in the representation
(\bm{1},\bm{2},\bm{1},\bm{2},\bm{1})\oplus(\bm{1},\bm{1},\bm{2},\bm{2},\bm{1})
of
G^{\mr{phys}}
. They can be pulled back to representations of
G^{\mr{tw}}
. Again, simple group theory arguments show that there is a supercharge
\s{Q}
singlet under
G^{\mr{tw}}
. The supersymmetry algebra implies that

{\cal Q}^{2}=-\frac{1}{2}Z~,

(112)

where
Z
is the generator of the central charge in the physical theory (the action of which on fields is given in Table
6
; fields of the vectormultiplet are neutral under
Z
). Using the action of supercharges in the physical theory, we obtain the action of
{\cal Q}
on
\Phi^{\mr{tw}}
and on the connections in Table
5
.

One should think of
{\cal Q}
as an odd vectorfield on the supermanifold of all fields, background, and dynamical. There is a sub-supermanifold where the background fermions are set to zero, and we will write down the action of
{\cal Q}
on this submanifold.
There is no off-shell formulation of an untwisted
{\cal N}=2
hypermultiplet with a finite number of auxiliary fields, but there exists one for the twisted hypermultiplet
KR (88)
.
29
29

29

The fact that the untwisted off-shell
{\cal N}=2
hypermultiplet requires infinitely many auxiliary fields is perhaps most elegantly seen in the formalism of projective superspace
LR (90)
;
LR (08)
: here, the hypermultiplet superfield is an infinite series in a twistorial coordinate
\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, with infinitely many coefficients corresponding to the infinitely many auxiliary fields.
The off-shell twisted
{\cal N}=2
hypermultiplet with a
finite
number of auxiliary fields was given in
KR (88)
, the authors having arrived at it by explicit construction. It would be interesting to understand this from a projective superspace viewpoint, but we leave this for future work. We thank M. Roček for a discussion about this point. Finally, we note that among the two presentations of the off-shell twisted
{\cal N}=2
hypermultiplet transformations in the book
LMn (05)
, their equations (5.51) do not close off-shell: in their language, the supersymmetry variation
\delta
satisfies
\delta^{2}\varphi\not\sim\varphi
and, in fact,
\delta^{4}\varphi\sim\varphi
for a typical hypermultiplet field
\varphi
. Their second presentation, namely equations (5.56), does close off-shell, and that is what we use.
One introduces spinorial, Grassmann-even auxiliary fields
h^{\alpha}
and
\widetilde{h}^{\alpha}

KR (88)
;
LMn (05)
;
HPP (95)
.
The
{\cal Q}
-action on fields is:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} [{\cal Q},\phi]&=0~,&[{\cal Q},\widetilde{\phi}]&=\eta~,&\{{\cal Q},\eta\}&=[\phi,\widetilde{\phi}]~,\\
\left[{\cal Q},A_{\mu}\right]&=\psi_{\mu}~,&\left\{{\cal Q},\psi_{\mu}\right\}&=-D_{\mu}\phi~,&\left[{\cal Q},{\cal A}^{u}_{\mu}\right]&=0~,\\
\left\{{\cal Q},\chi_{\mu\nu}\right\}&=F_{\mu\nu}^{+}-D_{\mu\nu}~,&\left[{\cal Q},D_{\mu\nu}\right]&=2\big(D_{[\mu}\psi_{\nu]}\big)^{+}-\left[\phi,\chi_{\mu\nu}\right]~,&\left[{\cal Q},\omega_{\mu}\right]&=0~,\\
\big[{\cal Q},M_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\big]&=\widetilde{\mu}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}~,&\big[{\cal Q},\widetilde{M}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\big]&=\widetilde{\lambda}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}~,\\
\left\{{\cal Q},\widetilde{\mu}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\right\}&=mM_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}-\phi M_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}~,&\big\{{\cal Q},\widetilde{\lambda}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\big\}&=-m\widetilde{M}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}+\widetilde{M}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\phi~,\\
\left\{{\cal Q},\lambda_{\alpha}\right\}&=h_{\alpha},&\left\{{\cal Q},\mu_{\alpha}\right\}&=\widetilde{h}_{\alpha}~,\\
[{\cal Q},h_{\alpha}]&=m\lambda_{\alpha}-\phi\lambda_{\alpha}~,&[{\cal Q},\widetilde{h}_{\alpha}]&=-m\mu_{\alpha}+\mu_{\alpha}\phi~.\end{aligned}

(113)

The central charge acts as multiplication by
\pm m
on the hypermultiplet fields. The
\pm 1
factors in the action of
Z,\overline{Z}
on various hypermultiplet fields are shown in Table
6
.

on various fields is given by the listed signs multiplying

.

From (
113
),
\mathcal{Q}
squares to a gauge transformation plus a central charge transformation:

{\cal Q}^{2}=-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{m}^{\mr{central-charge}}+\delta_{\phi}^{\mr{gauge}}~,

(114)

where the subscript
m
is the parameter (a mass) for the central charge transformation.

To formulate an action for the twisted theory, we begin with the action for the physical theory with fields
\Phi^{\mr{phys}}
,
\tau
,
m
and
\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{phys}}
and then specialize the connection
\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{phys}}
to the pushforward of
\bm{\nabla}^{\mr{tw}}
and express
\Phi^{\mr{phys}}
in terms of
\Phi^{\mr{tw}}
.
Here
A_{\mu}
and
{\cal A}_{\mu}^{u}
are YM and
\mr{U(1)}
connections respectively, while
\omega_{\mu}
,
\left(\omega_{+}\right)_{\mu}
are gravitational connections. A sensible way to formulate the action is in terms of a
\left(G^{\mr{gauge}}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}\right)
-gauge theory with matter coupled to background
\mathcal{N}=2
conformal supergravity (where the
{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}
vectormultiplet is treated as a non-dynamial background vectormultiplet), with fermions in the supergravity multiplet and
{\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}
-vectormultiplet set to zero (to ensure a
{\cal Q}
-exact energy-momentum tensor).
Note that
{\cal A}_{\mu}^{u}
is a
\mr{U(1)}/p_{3,u}(C^{\mr{phys}})
-connection, and
p_{3,u}(C^{\mr{phys}})\cong\mathbb{Z}/n_{u}\mathbb{Z}
. Thus,

{\cal A}_{\mu}^{u}=\frac{1}{n_{u}}B_{\mu}^{u}~,

(115)

where
B_{\mu}^{u}
is a
\mr{U(1)}
-connection in the
\mr{U(1)}_{u}
-factor of the

-vectormultiplet.

30

The rescalings are chosen to make contact with the vectormultiplet action of

\phi_{u}=-m_{u},\quad\widetilde{\phi}_{u}=\frac{m_{u}}{2}~,\quad u=1,\ldots,\mr{rank}\big({\mathbb{T}}^{\mr{f}}\big)~.

(116)

Finally choose generators

\s{S}^{\mr{twisted}}_{{\cal N}=2+\mr{bck}}=\s{S}^{\mr{twisted}}_{\mr{vec}}+\s{S}^{\mr{twisted}}_{\mr{hyp}}+\s{S}^{\mr{top}}~,

\displaystyle\hskip-14.45377pt\s{S}^{\mr{twisted}}_{\mr{vec}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\left.\eta^{a}D_{\mu}\psi^{\mu,b}+\widetilde{\phi}^{a}D_{\mu}D^{\mu}\phi^{b}-\frac{1}{2}\phi^{a}[\eta,\eta]^{b}-\frac{1}{2}[\phi,\widetilde{\phi}]^{a}[\phi,\widetilde{\phi}]^{b}-\widetilde{\phi}^{a}\left[\psi_{\mu},\psi^{\mu}\right]^{b}\right]~,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad-2m\widetilde{M}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\widetilde{\phi}M^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}+\widetilde{\lambda}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\left(\widetilde{\sigma}^{\rho\sigma}\right)^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}M_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}\chi_{\rho\sigma}+\eta\widetilde{\lambda}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}M^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}-\widetilde{M}^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\widetilde{\sigma}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}^{\rho\sigma}\widetilde{\mu}^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}\chi_{\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad-\eta\widetilde{M}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\widetilde{\mu}^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}+\lambda^{\alpha}\sigma^{\mu}_{\alpha\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}D_{\mu}\widetilde{\lambda}^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}-\widetilde{M}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\left(\widetilde{\sigma}^{\rho}\right)^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}\alpha}\lambda_{\alpha}\psi_{\rho}+\mu^{\alpha}\sigma_{\alpha\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}^{\rho}M^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}\psi_{\rho}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad+\mu^{\alpha}\sigma^{\rho}_{\alpha\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}D_{\rho}\widetilde{\mu}^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}+\mu^{\alpha}\phi\lambda_{\alpha}-2\widetilde{\lambda}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\widetilde{\phi}\widetilde{\mu}^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}-m\mu^{\alpha}\lambda_{\alpha}-m\widetilde{\lambda}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\widetilde{\mu}^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad+\widetilde{M}_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\{\phi,\widetilde{\phi}\}M^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}\left.-\widetilde{M}_{(\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}F^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}M_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta})}+\widetilde{M}_{(\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}D^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta}}M_{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\beta})}\right]~,

(120)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.45377pt\s{S}^{\mr{top}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\mathsf{i}}{32\pi}\int_{{\mathbb{X}}}~\tau_{ab}(F^{a}\wedge F^{b})+\frac{\mathsf{i}}{32\pi}\int_{{\mathbb{X}}}~\tau_{uv}(F^{u}\wedge F^{v})~.

(121)

Here

\displaystyle D_{\mu}\widetilde{\phi}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\mu}\widetilde{\phi}+[A_{\mu},\widetilde{\phi}]~,

(122)

\displaystyle=\partial_{\mu}\phi+[A_{\mu},\phi]~,

\displaystyle D_{\rho}\widetilde{\mu}^{\accentset{\mbox{\large.}}{\alpha}}_{u}

(128)

where
\omega_{\mu}{}^{\underline{a}\,\underline{b}}
is the spin connection. The factor of
\frac{q_{u}}{n_{u}}
